...and marked with divine Blood, manifestly passed through the sword that formerly barred the way, and settled rejoicing into paradise.[^10684]

*Martyrion:*  
How wonderful art Thou in Thy saints, O Christ, who have loved Thee in faith! For being enriched by Thee, they pour forth streams of divine healings unto the world, and dry up the flooding of our passions.[^10685]

*Theotokion:*  
Them that were sick with sin thou didst heal, O Virgin, who didst give birth unto the Savior and Physician of all, Who was nailed upon the Tree of the Cross, O Pure One: and Who poured forth salvation unto our souls.[^10686]

*Canon to the Theotokos. Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray.[^10687]

*Troparion 1:*  
With all zeal I hasten unto thy protection, O All-Immaculate One, and lift up the eye of my soul: turn me not away, but protect me as thou art good, and deliver me, and wash away the filth of my transgressions.[^10688]

*Troparion 2:*  
Deadly venom was injected by sinful teeth; but do thou destroy it by the nails and the divine spear of Thine Offspring, Who for tender mercy suffered in the flesh for our sake, O only All-Hymned One.[^10689]

*Troparion 3:*  
Revive me who am slain in manner of life and corrupted by transgressions, O thou who didst give birth unto life eternal; and by spiritual renewal transform me unto incorruption, O divinely-blessed one.[^10690]

*Troparion 4:*  
From demonic malice and the wicked deeds of men deliver me, O Sovereign Lady, and quickly heal bodily and spiritual sickness, O thou who alone didst give birth unto the Physician of all flesh and sins, the Savior and Lord.[^10691]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
In the belly of the aquatic beast Jonah, having stretched forth his palms in the form of a cross, manifestly prefigured the saving Passion; wherefrom coming forth on the third day, he depicted the transcendent Resurrection of Christ God nailed in the flesh, Who by His three-day Resurrection enlightened the world.[^10692]

*Troparion 1:*  
The Cross was planted upon the Place of the Skull in the midst of the earth, and healed the wound that blossomed through a tree in the midst of paradise; for the Messiah Jesus, having appeared as the one righteous One in the midst of lawless thieves, raised all up together with Himself, and cast down into the abyss him that had fallen from...[^10693]

...the heights.[^10694]

*Troparion 2:*  
Having bent Thy divine and all-honorable Cross as a bow, O Christ, Thou didst shoot arrows against the murderer; and Thou didst drive the nails of Thy hands, O Master, into his wrathful and all-defiled heart, and didst put him to death utterly, and those slain by him Thou didst bring to life, O Compassionate One.[^10695]

*Martyrion:*  
By the streams of blood that flowed from the bodies of the holy sufferers, they quenched by the Spirit all the flame of idolatry, and watered the honorable furrows of the Church, and caused to grow an ear of corn of the hope and faith of salvation, whereby every soul is nourished through divine grace.[^10696]

*Martyrion:*  
The glorious suffering passion-bearers burned in disposition more than fire, when they were delivered over by the sentence of wicked judges to be burned with material fire; and they were preserved unwounded by the operation and grace of the Holy Spirit, Who crowned them that suffered lawfully.[^10697]

*Theotokion:*  
A sword pierced thy heart, O all-pure Maiden, beholding Thy Son outstretched upon the Cross, enduring sufferings, and slaying the serpent, the ruler of darkness; therefore weeping maternally thou didst magnify Him.[^10698]

*Canon to the Theotokos. Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the deep of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.[^10699]

*Troparion 1:*  
I departed from God, having lived prodigally, and became a wretched slave of pleasures, and was stripped of every divine virtue; but visit me, O Pure One.[^10700]

*Troparion 2:*  
I became a fugitive from the commandments given unto me, and having estranged myself from life, I drew near unto death; but guide me unto return, O all-pure Mother of God.[^10701]

*Troparion 3:*  
My life is deceitful, full of negligence; but let thy great and ineffable mercy, O Pure One, and the compassion of thy goodness prevail over my feeble mind.[^10702]

*Troparion 4:*  
O thou who didst give birth unto the Savior and Deliverer and All-Compassionate One: have compassion...[^10703]

...upon me, O Pure One, and save me, and deliver me from the evils that compass me about and mercilessly assault my weakness.[^10704]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The senseless decree of the impious tyrant shook the people, breathing forth threat and God-abhorred blasphemy; yet the three children were not dismayed by beastly wrath nor by consuming fire, but when a dew-bearing wind blew counter to the fire, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers and our God.[^10705]

*Troparion 1:*  
When Thou wast outstretched upon the Tree of the Cross as a grapevine, O Word of the Father, Thou didst drop the sweet new wine of the mystery, replacing the drunkenness of transgression and gladdening all that know Thee as God and Creator, suffering willingly, and saving them that sing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers and our God.[^10706]

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst endure a shameful crucifixion, O my Christ Jesus, putting an end to all reproach and sighing for men; and Thou didst taste of gall, taking away all malicious bitterness. Thou didst wound Thy hands, O Compassionate One, healing our spiritual wounds and commanding us to sing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers.[^10707]

*Martyrion:*  
By sufferings, O noble sufferers, ye acquired a life free from pain; therefore ye wholly lighten our sicknesses, having received grace from on high, O holy ones, to heal passions and drive away spirits; and ye stand before the faithful and save them that cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers.[^10708]

*Martyrion:*  
Ye stood at the judgment seat, O Martyrs, confessing Christ, Who for our sake received flesh like unto ours, yet without corruption; and being truly shown forth as imitators of His sufferings, ye endured the affliction of fire and all other torments, crying aloud in gladness: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers.[^10709]

*Theotokion:*  
"I am filled with grief beholding Thee, O Savior, suffering without righteousness; I am pierced in Thy ribs," the Theotokos, the only Sovereign Lady, cried aloud weeping and wailing; whom all we worthily call blessed, and piously cry: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers.[^10710]

*Canon to the Theotokos. Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into...[^10711]

...dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.[^10712]

*Troparion 1:*  
Thou art all light-bearing, having received the unwaning Light, O Virgin, and enlightening them that cry aloud unto thee in faith, O pure one: Blessed, O Most Pure One, is the fruit of thy womb.[^10713]

*Troparion 2:*  
That thou mayest show forth thy love for mankind and thy mercy, O Virgin, lead me up from the deep of evils who cry aloud: Blessed, O Most Pure One, is the fruit of thy womb.[^10714]

*Troparion 3:*  
Unto thee I flee, O Mother-Virgin, wounded by soul-corrupting arrows: encompass me wholly with thy prayer who cry aloud: Blessed, O Most Pure One, is the fruit of thy womb.[^10715]

*Troparion 4:*  
Deliver me, O Mother of the Savior, from the cruel captivity of subtle thoughts that grips me, and from sinful causes, that being ever saved I may glorify thee as is due.[^10716]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Bless, ye children, equal in number to the Trinity, God the Father, the Creator; hymn the Word Who came down and turned the fire into dew; and supremely exalt unto all ages the all-holy Spirit, Who gives life unto all.[^10717]

*Troparion 1:*  
Blessed is the Tree whereby the deceitful curse that was in Eden, which grew through the wicked eating of the tree, was utterly consumed; and Christ is lifted on high, the supremely glorified One, Who for tender mercy was pleased of His own will to be lifted up thereon.[^10718]

*Troparion 2:*  
Sacredly crossing his hands of old, the ever-glorious one, in blessing his grandsons, showed forth the image of the sacred Tree, whereby blessing was granted unto all who were cursed through the wicked eating of the tree and had slipped down into the deep of evils.[^10719]

*Troparion 3:*  
All mankind is restored, O Master, when Thou wast outstretched upon the Cross, and the legion of deceitful demons fell; and things divided receive union, and the dominion of Thy power and Thy might is supremely exalted unto the ages.[^10720]

*Martyrion:*  
Blessed glory, incorruptible food, and radiant mansions have ye now inherited, O divine sufferers of the Lord: and ye have been joined unto the heavenly ranks, and have now received with gladness the end of your ever-glorious hopes.[^10721]

*Martyrion:*  
Your courage shone forth brighter than the sun, O radiant sufferers of Christ...[^10722]

...sufferers, and by divine power darkened all the deceit of the devil, and piously enlightened the hearts of all the faithful unto the ages.[^10723]

*Theotokion:*  
A spiritual cloud, and tabernacle of sanctification, throne of God, and gate of light, candlestick, and Orient of the Word, do all we faithful call thee, O all-immaculate Virgin, who art the blessed Mother of Him Who created all things.[^10724]

*Canon to the Theotokos. Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant frantically kindle the furnace for the pious; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried aloud to the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^10725]

*Troparion 1:*  
The Almighty Lord, having dwelt within Thy womb, showed thee forth unto the faithful as a pillar of confirmation; fleeing unto whom, we are delivered from perils and distresses, and are set free from temptation, singing in unison: Ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages.[^10726]

*Troparion 2:*  
I have fallen away from a consecrated life, O Sovereign Lady, being uncontrollably dragged toward sin and enslaved by it, and I am stripped of the freedom that is in Christ and of true righteousness; yet despise me not, O treasury of salvation, who flee unto thee, naked and impoverished.[^10727]

*Troparion 3:*  
Let the petition of my prayer be directed unto the Lord Who came forth from thy womb, O Sovereign Lady: and may He deliver me from transgression of the commandments, and from condemnation and the legal curse; and may He wash away the defilement of my grievous transgressions, as He alone is merciful.[^10728]

*Troparion 4:*  
O all-immaculate Birthgiver of God, cleanse the wounds and sinful stumbling-blocks of my soul, washing them with the fount of streams that flowed from the ribs of Thy Son; for unto thee do I cry, and unto thee do I flee, and thee do I invoke, who art full of God's grace.[^10729]

*Then, "My soul magnifies the Lord:"*[^10730]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Death, which came upon our race through the eating from the tree, has been abolished today through the Cross; for the ancestral universal curse has been dissolved through the offshoot of the pure Mother of God, whom all the heavenly hosts magnify.[^10731]

*Troparion 1:*  
Most sacredly exalting Thee, O Compassionate One, we venerate Thy Cross, and the spear, the sponge, and the reed, and the holy nails driven into Thy hands and feet...[^10732]

...O Master, whereby we have found perfect remission and have been accounted worthy of the life of paradise.[^10733]

*Troparion 2:*  
Oh, how unjustly wast Thou condemned to be nailed, crucified upon the Tree, O Thou Who alone art the most righteous King of all! Seeking to justify all who with faith glorify Thy voluntary sufferings and dispensation, and who faithfully magnify Thee, O my Christ.[^10734]

*Martyrion:*  
Having surrendered their bodies whole-souledly unto torments, the glorious Martyrs endured wounds and violent death, the severing of limbs, lacerations, and burning by fire, being inflamed with love for the Lord. Therefore, as crown-bearers, they dwell in the heavens.[^10735]

*Martyrion:*  
O Sweetness of Apostles and Martyrs, by the prayers of them all fill us with mercy, as Thou art compassionate, granting remission of sins, and deliverance from all grievous things, and dwelling in Thy Kingdom, O Christ God, Who didst appear as man for our sake.[^10736]

*Theotokion:*  
Thou didst appear as a light-formed bridal chamber, O Virgin, unto Him Who dwelt in thy incorrupt womb, and Who of His own will endured the blessed Passion, and Who granted dispassion unto all according to His ineffable mercy; Whom worshipping with faith, we piously magnify thee.[^10737]

*Canon to the Theotokos. Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Heaven was amazed at this, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee as the Mother of God.[^10738]

*Troparion 1:*  
Truly, O Mother of God, cease not to pray unto the existing God Whom thou didst bear, that He may grant remission of sins unto thy servants, and all-perfect forgiveness for the evil deeds now wrought by them, and account them worthy of eternal delight together with all the saints.[^10739]

*Troparion 2:*  
With might and spiritual strength, and with armor and dominion, gird my humble soul, O all-holy Theotokos, and clothe me with the weapon of the Cross; and cleanse the wounds of my sin with the dew of thy love for mankind and with thy great mercy.[^10740]

*Troparion 3:*  
Be thou unto me a pillar of salvation, O Pure One: render idle the regiments of demons, driving away the tumult of assaults and perils, and chasing far off the incursions of passions...[^10741]

...and granting pure liberation.[^10742]

*Troparion 4:*  
O pure, all-glorified Mother of God, save them that hymn thee with love, mercifully dispersing the tumult of temptations; for as thou didst give birth unto God, O Virgin, whatsoever thou desirest, thou art able to do, and thou dispensest mercy unhindered; therefore do all we magnify thee.[^10743]

*It is truly meet: litany, Exaposteilarion, and the customary psalms.*[^10744]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Cross, Tone 8:*[^10745]

*Sticheron 1:*  
The rod of Moses prefigured Thy precious Cross, O our Savior; for thereby Thou savest Thy people, as from the depth of the sea, O Lover of mankind.[^10746]

*Verse: We were filled in the morning with Thy mercy, O Lord: - - - And look upon Thy servants, and upon Thy works, and guide their sons. [Ps. 89:14, 16]*[^10747]

*Sticheron 2:*  
Of old in Eden, in paradise, a tree good for food sprouted in the midst of the orchard; but Thy Church, O Christ, blossomed forth Thy Cross, pouring forth life unto all the world. But that tree indeed brought death through food unto Adam who ate thereof, whereas this Tree made alive the thief who was saved through faith. Make us also partakers of his salvation, O Christ God, Who by Thy Passion didst destroy the enemy's wiles against us, and account us worthy of Thy Kingdom, O Lord.[^10748]

*Verse: And let the brightness of the Lord our God be upon us: - - - and direct the works of our hands upon us; yea, direct Thou the work of our hands. [Ps. 89:17]*[^10749]

*Martyrion:*  
What shall we call you, O saints? Cherubim? For Christ rested upon you; Seraphim? For unceasingly ye glorified Him; Angels? For ye turned away from the body; Powers? For ye work with miracles. Many are your names, and greater are your gifts. Pray that our souls may be saved.[^10750]

*Glory, both now: Stavrotheotokion:*  
O most marvelous wonder! O awesome mystery! How hangs He upon the Tree Who is immortal by nature? How does He now taste of death? How is the innocent One condemned? Hide thy light and be dismayed, O sun, beholding such audacity, the Virgin cried aloud with sighing, beholding Christ crucified, Whom she had borne.[^10751]

*It is good to give praise: the Trisagion, and the rest as usual.*[^10752]

*On Friday, the Beatitudes, Tone 8:*[^10753]

*Troparion 1:*  
Remember us, O Christ, Savior of the world, as Thou didst remember the thief upon the Tree; and account all worthy, O Thou Who alone art compassionate, of Thy heavenly Kingdom.[^10754]

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst stretch forth Thy palms upon the Tree, O Christ, and having exposed the wicked principalities and powers, Thou didst save from their injury them that piously glorify Thee.[^10755]

*Troparion 3:*  
Pierced with a spear while hanging upon the Tree, Thou didst pour forth unto us streams of immortality, who were senselessly put to death by transgression; therefore with fear we glorify Thee.[^10756]

*Martyrion:*  
Showing themselves strangers to the introduction of earthly pleasures, the sufferers surrendered themselves unto strange torments, wounding the apostate by their holy wounds.[^10757]

*Glory:*  
Being One of the unoriginate Trinity, Thou didst endure the Passion upon the Cross of Thine own will; dry up all the torrents of my passions, and account me worthy of salvation.[^10758]

*Both now:*  
Beholding Emmanuel, the Lamb of God and the Word, hanging in the flesh upon the Tree, the only undefiled Ewe-lamb and Virgin was gripped with sorrow, shedding tears.[^10759]

## Saturday: All Saints and the Departed[^10760]

*On Friday Evening.*[^10761]

*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, and Venerables, Tone 8:*[^10762]

*Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you, O holy ones" (Что̀ ва́съ нарече́мъ):*[^10763]

*Sticheron 1:*  
I, wretched one, am ever wounded by the arrows of the enemy, and what shall I do, I am at a loss. But ye who suffered valiantly broke his arrows, and by the effusion of your blood sanctify all creation; heal me who am diseased, O divine Martyrs, as ye have boldness before the Lord, O right-blessed ones.[^10764]

*Sticheron 2:*  
I am wholly gripped with perplexity when I consider that dreadful hour of Thine examination, O Judge of all and God, and I weep and lament, and...[^10765]

...wailing as I remember the abyss of my evils; therefore save me, O Lover of mankind, by the supplications of Thy martyrs, and deliver me from torment, as Thou art compassionate.[^10766]

*Sticheron 3:*  
For them that with faith celebrate your all-sacred memory, pray unto God, O divine Martyrs, that they may be delivered from temptations and attain heavenly mansions; and those whom Thou hast translated, as Thou alone art Benefactor and Lover of mankind, ordain their souls, O Long-suffering One, where the ranks of the Patriarchs ever rejoice.[^10767]

*Other Stichera, to the Martyrs, in the same Tone:*[^10768]

*Sticheron 4:*  
O Martyrs of the Lord, every place do ye sanctify, and every disease do ye heal; and now pray that our souls may be delivered from the snares of the enemy, we entreat you.[^10769]

*Sticheron 5:*  
O Martyrs of the Lord, pray unto our God, and entreat for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of many transgressions, we pray.[^10770]

*Sticheron 6:*  
O invincible Martyrs of Christ, having conquered deceit by the power of the Cross and received the grace of eternal life, ye feared not the threats of the tyrants, but rejoiced when wounded with torments; and now your blood has become healings for our souls. Pray that our souls may be saved.[^10771]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion (Dogmatikon):*  
The King of heaven, for His love of mankind, appeared upon earth and lived among men; for having taken flesh from the pure Virgin, and having come forth from her with the assumption, He is one Son, twofold in nature, but not in hypostasis. Therefore, truly preaching Him as perfect God and perfect man, we confess Christ our God. Whom beseech, O unwedded Mother, that our souls may be saved.[^10772]

*Then, "O Joyful Light:" and the rest.*[^10773]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 8:*[^10774]

*Sticheron 1:*  
Thy Martyrs, O Lord, forgetting the things of this life and disregarding torments for the sake of the life to come, were shown forth as heirs thereof; therefore they also rejoice with the Angels. By their prayers grant unto Thy people great mercy.[^10775]

*Sticheron 2:*  
If there be any virtue, and if there be any praise, it is meet for the Saints; for they bowed their necks to the sword for Thy sake, Who didst bow the heavens and come down; they shed their blood for Thy sake, Who didst empty Thyself and take the form of a servant;...[^10776]

...they humbled themselves even unto death, imitating Thy poverty. By their prayers, according to the multitude of Thy compassions, O God, have mercy upon us.[^10777]

*Sticheron for the Departed:*  
I weep and lament when I contemplate death, and behold our beauty created according to the image of God lying in the tombs, formless, inglorious, and without shape. O wonder! What is this mystery that has come to pass concerning us? How have we been surrendered unto corruption? How have we been yoked unto death? Truly by the command of God, as it is written, Who grants rest unto them that are translated.[^10778]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*  
O Sovereign Lady, receive the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and sorrow.[^10779]

*Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart: and the rest as usual.*[^10780]

*On Friday at Small Compline.*[^10781]

*Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 8.*[^10782]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea; for He alone is gloriously glorified.[^10783]

*Refrain: Most holy Lady Theotokos, save us.*[^10784]

*Troparion 1:*  
Put to death the carnal mind of my flesh, O all-pure Virgin who didst give birth unto Life, and deliver me from every torment.[^10785]

*Troparion 2:*  
With the most radiant ray of Him Who shone forth from thee, O Virgin, enlighten the pupils of my soul, that I may glorify thee.[^10786]

*Glory:*  
Pray, O Sovereign Lady, only holy Theotokos, that on the day of judgment I may obtain salvation and divine radiance.[^10787]

*Both now:*  
O all-pure Theotokos, soothe the pains of my heart, who didst bear Christ without pain, beyond understanding and expression.[^10788]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Thou art the confirmation of them that flee unto Thee; Thou art the Light of them that are darkened; and my spirit hymns Thee.[^10789]

*Troparion 1:*  
Deliver me by thy prayers, O Most Pure One, from the arrows of the enemy; and spare my heart from assaulting thoughts.[^10790]

*Troparion 2:*  
Heal the wounds of my soul, O All-Immaculate One; O Mother of God, calm the turmoil of my heart by thy prayers.[^10791]

*Glory:*  
Subdue the unrest of my thoughts, O pure Sovereign Lady, and take away all sorrow from my soul, who didst give birth unto Joy...[^10792]

*Both now:*  
Rejoice, salvation of them that flee unto thee, O Virgin-Mother of Christ. Rejoice, boast of Apostles and Martyrs.[^10793]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.[^10794]

*Troparion 1:*  
Heal the wounds of my soul, O Word of God, Who wast wounded for my sake as Lover of mankind, and enlighten the darkness of my mind.[^10795]

*Troparion 2:*  
Through the slumber of my negligence, the sleep of sin overtook me, O Virgin: by thy watchful prayer raise me up unto repentance.[^10796]

*Glory:*  
O All-Immaculate One, who didst pour forth the water of remission from founts: water my heart that is dried up by all manner of transgressions.[^10797]

*Both now:*  
O most holy Bride of God, Sovereign Lady of the world: save me, changing misfortunes and driving away the tumult of passions.[^10798]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Rising early, we cry aloud unto Thee: Lord, save us, for Thou art our God; besides Thee we know no other.[^10799]

*Troparion 1:*  
Look down, O Most Pure One, and hear my voice, and cleanse all mine iniquities, O Sovereign Lady.[^10800]

*Troparion 2:*  
O Theotokos, who didst give birth unto the unwaning Sun: enlighten me wholly who am darkened by passions, that I may glorify and praise thee, O All-Immaculate One.[^10801]

*Glory:*  
Have compassion upon my soul, O most holy Maiden, and deliver me from judgment and from eternal torment.[^10802]

*Both now:*  
Heal my ailing soul, O most holy Sovereign Lady, who didst give birth unto Him Who takes away the diseases of all.[^10803]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
An abyss of sins and a storm of transgressions troubles me, and thrusts me into the deep of violent despair; but stretch forth Thy strong hand unto me as unto Peter, O Helmsman, and save me.[^10804]

*Troparion 1:*  
Put to death, O Theotokos, the disorderly motions of my flesh, who didst make alive those slain by malice through thy living Nativity; that I may glorify thee as the cause of mankind's renewal.[^10805]

*Troparion 2:*  
O Theotokos Virgin, who didst give birth unto an abyss of compassions: save my soul from the sorrows of this life, and open unto me the doors of spiritual joy; for in thee alone have I placed my hope.[^10806]

*Glory:*  
That I may joyfully hymn thy greatness, O all-pure Virgin, and...[^10807]

...the great grace of miracles, do thou alter by thy prayers the unseemly thoughts that ever afflict me.[^10808]

*Both now:*  
Beyond nature didst thou give birth unto Him Who is unparted from the Father's bosom, and Who through thee, O Virgin, conversed with men. But as thou art the boast and confirmation of us all, O Theotokos, have compassion upon them that flee unto thee.[^10809]

*Then, "Lord, have mercy" (40). Glory, both now:*[^10810]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^10811]  
The Father's Word came down upon the earth, and a radiant Angel said unto the Theotokos: Rejoice, O blessed one, who alone hast preserved thy chamber, having received the conception of the pre-eternal God and Lord, that as God He might save the human race from deceit.[^10812]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.[^10813]

*Troparion 1:*  
Woe is me! How shall I escape the torments that await me, having lived evilly here? How shall I, wretched one, appear before the awesome Judge? O Birthgiver of God, Sovereign Lady, be my helper then.[^10814]

*Troparion 2:*  
Quench the flame of my passions, and calm the storm of my heart, O pure Mother of God: and deliver me, O All-Immaculate One, from the torment of demons and from eternal fire.[^10815]

*Glory:*  
Behold my affliction, O Virgin, which the multitude of mine evils hath brought on; and grant me rest before my departure, entreating Thy Son by thy maternal prayers.[^10816]

*Both now:*  
Quench the flame of my heart's passions with thy dew, O Virgin, and rescue me, O Maiden, from the fearful fire, from eternal judgment, and from demonic torment.[^10817]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Bless God the Father, the Creator, ye children equal in number to the Trinity; hymn the Word Who came down and transformed the fire into dew; and supremely exalt unto all ages the all-holy Spirit, Who gives life unto all.[^10818]

*Troparion 1:*  
Put to death, O Virgin, all our carnal motions, who didst make the serpent dead by thy life-bearing Nativity; and pray that we may attain the life that waxes not old, that we may hymn thee unto the ages.[^10819]

*Troparion 2:*  
Thee, O Birthgiver of God, do I ever invoke unto aid: do not...[^10820]

...put me to shame who have acquired deeds of shame, but have compassion upon me, O Most Pure One, rescue me from the flame, and deliver me from eternal torments.[^10821]

*Glory:*  
O full of grace, appear unto me as helper in the distress of evils, delivering me from the assaults of demonic wiles that come upon me, that I may ever bless thee, O Theotokos, mediatress of all.[^10822]

*Both now:*  
O most holy Maiden, boast of Apostles, glory of Martyrs: make me a partaker of eternal glory, who cry aloud: Bless the all-holy Spirit unto all the ages.[^10823]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Thou didst transcend the laws of nature, having conceived the Creator and Lord, and wast the door of salvation unto the world; therefore we unceasingly magnify thee, O Theotokos.[^10824]

*Troparion 1:*  
By thine unwaning light, O gate of light, drive away the cruel darkness of my soul, and show unto me the light-bearing paths of repentance, that walking therein, I may escape the murky darkness of sin and unceasingly magnify thee.[^10825]

*Troparion 2:*  
Cleanse Thy servants, O Good One, and grant forgiveness of transgressions; and deliver us from eternal flame, and make us partakers of Thy Kingdom, O Word of God; for desiring to save the human race, Thou didst endure the Cross.[^10826]

*Glory:*  
Grant me, O Theotokos, easily to pass through the journey of this life, and as thou art good, subdue the uprisings of assaults and passions, and guide me unto the virtue of the heavenly Kingdom and divine rest, that being saved, I may glorify thee.[^10827]

*Both now:*  
Heal my soul that hath suffered with grievous passion, as thou art merciful, who didst give birth unto the all-good God; and deliver me, O Most Pure One, from them that ever afflict me and from grievous things that increase, that being saved, I may fervently magnify thee who hast magnified our race.[^10828]

*It is truly meet: the Trisagion; after "Our Father:" the troparion and dismissal.*[^10829]

*On Saturday at Matins.*[^10830]

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymns of the Martyrs, Tone 8:*[^10831]

*Sessional Hymn 1:*  
With heavenly light this temple is enlightened today, for in it the angelic hosts rejoice, and together with them the choirs of martyrs make merry in the memory of the passion-bearers. By their prayers send down, O Christ, unto our souls...[^10832]

...peace and great mercy.[^10833]

*Verse: God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel. [Ps. 67:36]*[^10834]

*Sessional Hymn 2:*  
Spiritual luminaries were ye shown forth, O holy Martyrs, for by faith ye abolished the darkness of deceit, and enlightened your spiritual lamps, and with the Bridegroom entered in glory into the heavenly bridal chamber; and now we entreat you: Pray that our souls may be saved.[^10835]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord. [Ps. 64:5]*[^10836]

*Sessional Hymn 3, For the Departed:*  
Numbered among the righteous in the mansions of the elect, O Master, those who have piously departed from temporary things: giving them rest in the places of those that keep festival and in unending paradisaical delight, as Thou art good, forgiving their voluntary and involuntary transgressions according to Thine utmost goodness, O Lover of mankind.[^10837]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*  
Thou Who for our sake wast born of the Virgin, and didst endure crucifixion, O Good One, Who by death didst overthrow death, and didst manifest the Resurrection as God: despise not them whom Thou hast fashioned with Thine own hand; show forth Thy love for mankind, O Merciful One; accept the Theotokos who bare Thee, who intercedes for us, and save, O our Savior, a despairing people.[^10838]

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymns, Tone 8:*[^10839]

*Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости наста́вниче):*[^10840]

*Sessional Hymn 1:*  
Ever-radiant luminaries unto the universe were ye shown forth through faith, having placed all your hope martyrlike upon the Lord; and with the spiritual oil of the Holy Spirit ye enlightened your spiritual lamps. Therefore also as spiritual cups that dispense healings unto men, even as waters, were ye shown forth, O all-praised passion-bearers; pray unto Christ God that He grant remission of transgressions unto them that lovingly honor your holy memory.[^10841]

*Verse: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath been wonderful; all His desires are in them. [Ps. 15:3]*[^10842]

*Sessional Hymn 2:*  
O Prophets, Martyrs of Christ, and Hierarchs, who piously and lawfully finished the good fight and received unfading crowns from God: unceasingly pray unto His goodness for us, that He may grant us forgiveness of transgressions, as He is a God Who is easily entreated.[^10843]

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things. [Ps. 24:13]*[^10844] *Sessional Hymn 3, For the Departed:*
Who with profound wisdom orderest all things in philanthropy, and dispensest unto all that which is profitable, O only Creator: give rest, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy servants; for they have placed their hope in Thee, our Creator, Fashioner, and God.[^10845]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*  
In thee do we have a wall and a haven, and an acceptable intercessor before God Whom thou didst bear, O unwedded Theotokos, salvation of the faithful.[^10846]

*Canon to the Holy Martyrs, Hierarchs, Venerables, and the Departed. (Whose acrostic is this: The divine end of the new Octoechos.) The labor of Joseph.*[^10847]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea; for He alone is gloriously glorified.[^10848]

*Refrain: Holy Martyrs, pray unto God for us.*[^10849]

*Troparion 1:*  
Precious pearls were ye shown forth, adorning the crown of the honorable Church, O most valiant passion-bearers of Christ.[^10850]

*Refrain: Hierarchs of Christ, pray unto God for us.*[^10851]

*Troparion 2:*  
The all-wise hierarchs shone forth with the divine splendors of dogmas and virtues, and enlightened the hearts of the faithful.[^10852]

*Refrain: All ye Saints, pray unto God for us.*[^10853]

*Troparion 3:*  
Thee Who art marvelous in the prophets and the venerables do we entreat, O Word: by their supplications save us.[^10854]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
When Thou comest to work righteous judgment, O most Righteous One, without condemnation, O Word, save us by their supplications.[^10855]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Knowing that she bare the Lord, the choir of women, having suffered martyrdom after thee, O Virgin, was brought unto Him.[^10856]

*Second Canon, of the Departed. The work of Theophanes.*[^10857]

*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Having passed through the water as upon dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God.[^10858]

*Refrain: Wonderful is God in His saints, the God of Israel. [Ps. 67:36]*[^10859]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrion):*  
By death having imitated the death of Christ, and by sufferings His honorable passion, all the martyrs obtained the divine and blessed life.[^10860]

*Refrain: Give rest, O Lord, unto the souls of Thy departed servants.*[^10861]

*Troparion 2:*  
Overlooking the offenses of their youth, and remitting their sins, O Christ the Savior, number Thy servants who departed before...[^10862]

...with Thine elect.[^10863]

*Glory:*  
The glory and joy which they who have acquired the blessed abode have obtained, grant richly unto Thy servants whom Thou hast received, O Many-merciful One.[^10864]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Thou didst conceive the Father's Word, Which was united to the flesh according to hypostasis, Which from thee, O all-immaculate Maiden, abolished hades by divine power.[^10865]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Thou Who didst establish the heavens by the Word, and didst found the earth upon many waters, establish me unto the singing of Thy doxology, O Lord.[^10866]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
Having cast off abominable sacrifices with strongest resolve, the sufferers became a purest sacrifice unto the Word Who was sacrificed.[^10867]

*Troparion 2 (Hierarchs):*  
Renewing with sacredly-working words those grown old through all manner of passions, ye were shown forth as divine disciples of Him Who renewed the world.[^10868]

*Troparion 3 (Venerables):*  
The grace of the All-Holy Spirit given unto the prophets aforetime manifestly filled the ascetics in the last times with divine gifts.[^10869]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
Unite unto the choirs of the Saints, O God, them that have faithfully departed from life, and make them dwellers of paradise, through Thine ineffable mercy.[^10870]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
He Who is nowise containable, Jesus and the Lord, dwelt without being straitened in thy sanctified womb, O all-pure, all-hymned Virgin.[^10871]

*Canon of the Departed. Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.[^10872]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrion):*  
Having cleansed yourselves from the ancient ancestral fall by baptism and regeneration, and having sprinkled yourselves with the streams of your own blood, O blessed ones, ye reign together with Christ.[^10873]

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou Who willingly wast laid dead in the tomb, and Who callest forth them that dwell in the tombs, O Savior: be well pleased that those whom Thou hast received from us should dwell in the mansions of Thy righteous ones.[^10874]

*Glory:*  
Entreated by the loving-kindness of Thy divine goodness, O Master, and by that goodness which is known as co-natural with Thee, giving remission of sins unto Thy servants...[^10875]

...O Savior, give rest.[^10876]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Incarnate from thy womb and having become man, He Who is pre-eminently the only Lover of mankind saves man from the gates of death, O all-pure, only all-hymned Mother of God.[^10877]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.[^10878]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
Having run the course of martyrdom with spiritual warmth, O divinely blessed sufferers, ye burned up material deceit.[^10879]

*Troparion 2 (Hierarchs and Venerables):*  
Thy Venerables, O Lord, and Hierarchs hast Thou radiantly glorified; by their divine supplications make me a partaker of Thy glory.[^10880]

*Troparion 3 (Holy Women):*  
The inspiration of the Divine Spirit which enlightened the prophets granted strength unto women to destroy the insolence of enemies.[^10881]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
Vouchsafe Thy servants whom Thou hast translated, O All-Good One, being entreated, to join the choir together with all the Saints in the mansions of Thy holy ones.[^10882]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Above the law of nature didst thou give birth unto the Giver of the divine law, Who rebuilds fallen human nature, O All-Immaculate One.[^10883]

*Canon of the Departed. Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power; Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who left not the bosom of the Father, and visited our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.[^10884]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrion):*  
That they might behold Thy glory, O Master, and radiantly obtain Thy light-giving splendor in the heavens, the divine martyrs endured to bear every manner of torments, singing unto Thee, O Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.[^10885]

*Troparion 2:*  
Many mansions are with Thee, O Savior, distributed unto all according to their worth, according to the measure of their virtues: vouchsafe, therefore, O Compassionate One, that these who have departed in faith may fill them, piously crying unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.[^10886]

*Glory:*  
A man like unto us didst Thou appear, O Immortal One, and Thou didst endure death equally for all, and didst show the path of life; those who have departed...[^10887]

...vouchsafe them, as Lover of mankind, granting remission of sins as Master, and bestowing communion in light.[^10888]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Thou art the praise of the faithful, O unwedded Maiden. Thou art the protectress, thou art the refuge of Christians, a wall and a haven. Thou bearest supplications unto thy Son, O All-Immaculate One, and dost save from perils them that know thee with faith and love as the pure Theotokos.[^10889]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant us Thy peace, O only Lover of mankind.[^10890]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
The divine martyrs endured bodily wounds, and for this reason they ever heal our wounds, wounding the demons.[^10891]

*Troparion 2 (Hierarchs and Venerables):*  
Holy hierarchs of God, with all the venerables, pray unto Christ to grant us remission of sins.[^10892]

*Troparion 3 (Venerable Women):*  
Having worn out the flesh with wounds and ascetical discipline, O Lover of mankind, the venerable women were vouchsafed good hopes.[^10893]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
Having destroyed death by Thy death, O Immortal One: give rest, as Lover of mankind, unto Thy faithful servants who have died in hope of life.[^10894]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Thou didst abolish the condemnation of our foreparents, O Pure One, having borne in the flesh Jesus, the only Lord, Who justifies all.[^10895]

*Canon of the Departed. Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light never setting, and a strange darkness hath covered me, wretched one? But turn me, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray.[^10896]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrion):*  
Having shown compassion, vouchsafe, O only Lover of mankind, those who have been translated unto the dispassionate life to partake of Thine ineffable glory, and of Thy blessedness which surpasses words, in the mansions of the saints, where is the voice of them that keep festival.[^10897]

*Troparion 2:*  
Where the angelic ranks and where the assemblies of the righteous rejoice, in the bosom of Abraham settle Thy servants, O Savior; and be well pleased, O Merciful One, that with boldness they may stand before the awesome and divine throne.[^10898]

*Glory:*  
Purification wast Thou shown forth unto us, and righteousness and redemption; and by Thy wound Thou didst heal our infirmities. Do Thou, therefore, O Compassionate One...[^10899]

...as Thou art good, place those who have departed in the delight of paradise.[^10900]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Compassionately, O Merciful One, Thou didst take upon Thyself the measure of humanity; Thou Who adornest with highest exaltations of divine glory, having received from virginal loins ensouled, rational flesh, wherewith Thou didst destroy death.[^10901]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation.[^10902]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
By your honorable suppurations ye heal the sicknesses of souls, O holy martyrs, and ever take away the putrefactions of bodies, wounding the multitude of deceitful demons.[^10903]

*Troparion 2 (Venerables, Hierarchs, and Holy Women):*  
The choir of the venerables, the assembly of hierarchs, and the divine company of holy women, having firmly suffered, inherited heavenly delights.[^10904]

*Troparion 3 (Ascetics and Hierarchs):*  
Having put the flesh to death, O ascetics, ye obtained life; and having well pastured the flock of Christ, O all-wise hierarchs, ye were vouchsafed immortal glory after your end.[^10905]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
Being the life of the living and the rest of the dead, O Word: settle Thy departed servants, translated from among us by Thy divine nodding, in the bosom of Abraham Thy servant.[^10906]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
The Father's radiance dwelt in thee, O Pure One, and by the immaterial rays of His Divinity shattered the darkness of polytheism, and enlightened the world.[^10907]

*Canon of the Departed. The same Heirmos.*[^10908]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrion):*  
Thy martyrs, O Savior, endured many torments, being wounded in soul by Thy love, O holy ones, desiring Thine ever-existing glory and sweet communion.[^10909]

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou didst tear open the belly of the enemy by death, O Savior, and didst raise up all the prisoners that were in him, bestowing life; whereof vouchsafe those who have departed, O Benefactor.[^10910]

*Glory:*  
From tears and sighing free Thy servants who are in hades, O Savior: for as Thou alone art compassionate, Thou hast taken away every tear...[^10911]

...from every face of them that faithfully theologize Thee.[^10912]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
He Who is Creator by nature is created in thy womb; full as God, He empties Himself, O All-Immaculate One: the only Immortal One dies by death for the salvation of all.[^10913]

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*  
With the Saints give rest, O Christ, unto the souls of Thy servants, where there is no sickness, nor sorrow, nor sighing, but life unending.[^10914]

*Ikos:*  
Thou alone art immortal, Who didst make and fashion man; for we earthly were fashioned from the earth, and unto the same earth shall we go, as Thou didst command, Who didst fashion me and say unto me: Earth thou art, and unto earth shalt thou depart; whither all we mortals go, making our funeral lamentation the song: Alleluia.[^10915]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The godly Children in Babylon bowed not down unto the golden image, but bedewed in the midst of the fiery furnace, they sang a hymn, saying: O supremely exalted God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.[^10916]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
Having broken down the wall of impiety by your holy wounds, O all-praised ones: disperse the burden of my sins, and save him that cries, O Martyrs: Blessed is the God of our fathers.[^10917]

*Troparion 2 (Hierarchs):*  
With showers of your sacred blood ye quenched the fire of heresies, and with the flame, O all-wise Hierarchs, ye burned up the tares of impiety's deceit, and enlightened the souls of the faithful.[^10918]

*Troparion 3 (Ascetics, Prophets, and Holy Women):*  
Ascetics, having mortified the flesh by temperance, live after death; the righteous choir of prophets and the company of women that suffered martyrdom have been glorified. By their prayers, O Christ, deliver us from perils.[^10919]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
Give rest, O Christ, unto the souls of all that have fallen asleep in hope of life, overlooking their sins committed in life, according to Thine abundant loving-kindness, O compassionate, only Savior, God of the fathers.[^10920]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Wholly pure, thou didst receive into thy womb the incarnate Word; pray Him, O Most Pure One, to cleanse me of transgressions, of soul...[^10921]

...and body, of him that flees unto thee with pure faith.[^10922]

*Canon of the Departed. Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Hebrew Children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.[^10923]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrion):*  
All the desire of the martyrs was extended toward the one Master, being united unto Him with love, and singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.[^10924]

*Troparion 2:*  
Thou Who grantest the splendor of the divine Kingdom unto them that have departed in faith, bestow the garment of incorruption upon them that cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.[^10925]

*Glory:*  
Fill with joy and gladness Thy servants whom Thou hast translated, O Compassionate One, having vouchsafed them to cry unto Thee and sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.[^10926]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Loosing the curse of Eve, Thou didst dwell in the all-immaculate Virgin, pouring forth a fount of blessing unto them that cry: Blessed, O Most Pure One, is the fruit of thy womb.[^10927]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The divinely-speaking Children in the furnace, trampling upon the flame with fire, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord.[^10928]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
Suffering, ye cast down the idol temples, and made yourselves divine temples of the Trinity, O passion-bearers of the Lord, companions of the Angels.[^10929]

*Troparion 2 (Priests):*  
Thy priests, O Christ, having clothed themselves holily with righteousness, now rejoice with them that are from the age, beholding Thy divine beauty most clearly.[^10930]

*Troparion 3 (Prophets, Holy Women, and Righteous):*  
By the supplications of Thy most sacred Prophets, and of the ever-glorious women, and of the righteous from the age, grant Thy mercies unto Thy world, O Word.[^10931]

*Refrain: We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, the Lord.*[^10932]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
O Righteous Judge, when Thou desirest to judge those whom Thou hast received from us, preserve them uncondemned, overlooking their sins, O Master.[^10933]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Having appeared, O Virgin, drive away the darkest clouds of my soul with thine illumination, who didst give birth unto the Sun of Righteousness.[^10934]

*Canon of the Departed. Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
When the musical instruments sounded in harmony, and numberless peoples worshipped the image in Dura, the three Children, refusing to submit, sang hymns and doxologies unto the Lord unto all the ages.[^10935]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrion):*  
Having finished earthly struggles, the true martyrs received heavenly crowns, crying unto Thee unceasingly: Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all the ages.[^10936]

*Troparion 2:*  
Having descended into the nethermost pit, Thou didst resurrect them that dwell in the tombs with Thy life-originating palm, and didst grant rest, O Compassionate One, unto Thy servants who have fallen asleep before in faith.[^10937]

*Glory:*  
As Thou art a fount of ever-existing life, as Thou art a stream of delight: vouchsafe Thy servants who have piously departed unto Thee to hymn and doxologize Thee unto all the ages.[^10938]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Mary, Virgin Theotokos, who didst bear God the Savior unto men in the flesh: save them that sing thy Nativity in faith, and supremely exalt it unto all the ages.[^10939]

*Then: "My soul doth magnify the Lord:"*[^10940]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Thee, the Mother of the most high God without bridegroom, thee who didst bear God in truth by word beyond understanding, who art higher than the all-pure powers, with unceasing doxologies we magnify.[^10941]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
The martyrs stood before unjust judges, having condemned all unrighteousness by the grace of Christ: and delivering them that were held by them, and receiving the crowns of righteousness.[^10942]

*Troparion 2 (Hierarchs):*  
Ye were shown forth as the helms of the Church, having piously directed the whole ship unto the haven of God's commandments, all ye blessed hierarchs and pastors: therefore as helmsmen we honor you.[^10943]

*Troparion 3 (Prophets, Venerables, and Holy Women):*  
The assembly of Prophets and Venerables entreats Thee, O Lord; the company of women who suffered martyrdom most radiantly and who shone forth in ascetical discipline entreats Thee, O Lover of mankind: grant Thy compassions unto us.[^10944]

*Glory: For the Departed:*  
Of that honor and eternal glory wherewith all the saints of Christ have been vouchsafed: vouchsafe Thy servants who have faithfully departed from vain life to partake, O Merciful One, by the prayers of Thy saints.[^10945]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Carrying in thine embraces the fullness of good things, O All-Immaculate One, fulfill the supplications of us, thy servants: and direct our journeyings toward God, strengthening us to live virtuously.[^10946]

*Canon of the Departed. Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Every hearing was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, having become man from the virginal womb; therefore the faithful magnify the all-pure Theotokos.[^10947]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
Having an invincible and unconquerable strength, O martyrs of Christ, ye brought to nought the godless decree of the tyrants; and manifestly were ye vouchsafed the heavenly Kingdom, enlightened by Trinity-rays, O laudable ones.[^10948]

*Troparion 2:*  
Bitter hades was destroyed when Thou didst destroy it, O Lover of mankind, and didst resurrect those who had been sleeping there from the age; but now also, as Thou art good, vouchsafe those who have departed unto Thee, O Compassionate One, of Thine unwaning light.[^10949]

*Glory:*  
Thou art wholly sweetness, O Savior; Thou art wholly desire and love truly insatiable; Thou art wholly ineffable beauty: be well pleased, therefore, that those who have departed unto Thee may delight in Thy comeliness, and vouchsafe them of Thy divine beauty.[^10950]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Save me, O Mother of God, who didst bear Christ my Savior, God and man, twofold in nature and in hypostasis: only-begotten indeed of the Father, and from thee the firstborn of all creation. Whom in twofold nature we magnify.[^10951]

*It is truly meet: Litany, and Exapostilarion, and customary psalms.*[^10952]

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 8:*[^10953]

*Sticheron 1:*  
What shall we call you, O holy ones? Cherubim? For upon you Christ rested. Seraphim? For unceasingly ye glorified Him. Angels? For ye turned away from bodies. Powers? For ye work miracles. Many are your names, and greater your gifts. Pray that our souls may be saved.[^10954]

*Sticheron 2:*  
Mightily did ye contend, O holy ones, having valiantly endured the torments of the lawless, and confessed Christ before kings; and having departed from life, ye work powers in the world, and heal the sick of their passions, O holy ones, praying that our souls may be saved.[^10955]

*Sticheron 3:*  
Invincible martyrs of Christ, having conquered deceit by the power of the Cross, ye delight in the grace of eternal life; ye feared not the threats of the tyrants, and being wounded by torments ye made merry...[^10956]

...and now your blood has become healings unto our souls. Pray that our souls may be saved.[^10957]

*Sticheron 4:*  
Having well clad yourselves in the breastplates of faith, and armed yourselves with the sign of the Cross, ye were shown forth as valiant warriors; ye manfully opposed the tyrants, and cast down the deceit of the devil; having become victors, ye were vouchsafed crowns. Pray ever for us, that we may be saved.[^10958]

*Glory:*  
Numberless torment is for them that live profligately: gnashing of teeth, and comfortless weeping, fiery Gehenna, and outer darkness, and the sleepless worm, useless tears, and the unmerciful Judge. Therefore before the end we cry, saying: O Master Christ, give rest with Thine elect unto those whom Thou hast chosen.[^10959]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
O pure Virgin, door of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved.[^10960]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera for the Departed, Tone 8:*[^10961]

*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^10962]

*Sticheron 1:*  
A royal decree of freedom hast Thou signed for me, having reddened Thy fingers with scarlet ink, and stained them with Thine own blood, O Master; therefore in faith we entreat Thee: number with Thy firstborn, and vouchsafe to obtain the joy of Thy righteous ones, those who have passed over unto Thee, the Tenderhearted One.[^10963]

*Verse: Blessed are they whom Thou hast chosen and taken, O Lord. [Ps. 64:5]*[^10964]

*Sticheron 2:*  
Having exercised the priesthood as man, and having been slain as a lamb, Thou didst offer Thyself an oblation unto the Father, taking man out from corruption. Do Thou therefore, as Lover of mankind, settle those who have departed in the land of the living, where the streams of delight pour forth, where the fountains of eternal life gush out.[^10965]

*Verse: Their souls shall dwell among good things. [Ps. 24:13]*[^10966]

*Sticheron 3:*  
By the depth of Thine ineffable wisdom Thou limitest life, and foreseest the future, and translatest Thy servants unto another life; those whom Thou hast received, therefore, settle beside the waters of rest, in the splendor of the saints, O Lord, where is the voice of rejoicing and praise.[^10967]

*Verse: And their memory is from generation to generation. [Ps. 101:13]*[^10968]

*Sticheron 4:*  
Being the invisible Word, co-originate with the Father, and...[^10969]

...form, for my sake didst appear in the flesh as man; since therefore Thou art merciful and Lover of mankind, enlighten those who have passed over from this life with the beauties of Thy comeliness and loveliness, O most Life-Originating One.[^10970]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*  
As thou didst conceive the unoriginate Word of God the Father, by thy maternal boldness diligently pray, O Theotokos, to establish thy servants where is the unceasing rejoicing of the righteous, who make merry and praise thee; where is the everlasting brightness and the sweet voice of them that keep festival.[^10971]

*On Saturday, at the Liturgy, The Beatitudes, Tone 8:*[^10972]  
Remember us, O Christ, Savior of the world, as Thou rememberedst the thief upon the tree; and vouchsafe us all, O only Compassionate One, of Thy heavenly Kingdom.[^10973]

*Troparion 1 (Martyrs):*  
Firmly partaking of all afflictions, by your holy wounds, O passion-bearers, ye wounded all the dark hordes of demons by divine grace.[^10974]

*Troparion 2 (Hierarchs and Venerables):*  
The venerable of Christ and the divine hierarchs, the assembly of prophets, and all the holy righteous have obtained the heavenly inheritance; whom let us worthily call blessed.[^10975]

*Troparion 3 (For the Departed):*  
All who in faith have departed from this life, establish, O God, in the lands of the righteous; and show forth as heirs of paradise those who in a manner befitting God sing praises unto Thee.[^10976]

*Glory:*  
A final song I offer unto Thee, O Trinity: those whom Thou hast translated from the earth in faith, vouchsafe of the dwellings of the saints, and have mercy upon me, the prodigal.[^10977]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Having given birth to the fullness of all good things, fulfill our supplications, O Virgin: asking for us remission of offenses, and enlightenment, and great mercy.[^10978]

*End of Tone 8.*[^10979]

*Triadica of Gregory of Sinai, sung on all Sundays, after the Triadic Canon at the Midnight Office:*[^10980]

*Triadicon 1:*  
It is meet in a manner befitting God to glorify Thee, O Holy Trinity, boundless and transcendent, and our all-good God, Whom all the earth-born praise, and the heavenly Hosts glorify; Who art in faith worshipped by all, a Monad in essence, three in hypostases.[^10981]

*Triadicon 2:*  
Master-Origin of the Cherubim, and Divine Origin beyond compare of the Seraphim, indivisible Trinity in Unity, the existing Divine Principality: we magnify Thee.[^10982]

*Triadicon 3:*  
Unto the unoriginate Father and God, unto the co-unoriginate Word with the Spirit, I bow down. The inseparable single united essence, the threefold Unity, let us honor with hymns.[^10983]

*Triadicon 4:*  
Let us all sing in a manner befitting God with divine hymns unto the Father, and the Son, and the divine Spirit: the three-hypostatic dominion, the one Kingdom and Lordship.[^10984]

*Triadicon 5:*  
Make Thy beam-radiant lightnings to shine upon me, O my God, three-hypostatic Creator of all; and show me forth as a house of Thine unapproachable glory, bright, light-bearing, and unchanging.[^10985]

*Triadicon 6:*  
Before Whom the Cherubim tremble and quake, and Whom the angelic hosts glorify: Christ the Giver of life, Who was ineffably incarnate of the Virgin, let us praise with fear.[^10986]

*Triadicon 7:*  
It is meet in all reverent awe to pray unto Thee with tears, the All-Merciful and All-Compassionate One, and the Savior of our souls.[^10987]

*Triadicon 8:*  
Receive tears, O King; receive the weeping and lamentation of the wretched one; and deliver me from the flame and the outer darkness, and make me a partaker of the divine Kingdom.[^10988]

*Glory:*  
O Father Almighty, Word of God, All-Holy Spirit: one power...[^10989]

...and form, one Kingdom and Godhead and dominion: have mercy and save those who worship Thee.[^10990]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Having seen thy Son resurrected from the dead in a manner befitting God, O all-pure Virgin, creation was filled with ineffable joy; glorifying Him, we magnify thee.[^10991]

*Then: It is truly meet: Trisagion: after Our Father: the Hypakoe of the Tone, or the Troparia: Have mercy on us, O Lord, have mercy on us: Then the litany, and the dismissal.*[^10992]

*Troparia sung after the Blameless (Kathisma 17) on all Sundays, Tone 5:*[^10993]

*Refrain: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, teach me Thy statutes. [Ps. 118:12]*[^10994]

*Troparion 1:*  
The angelic host was astonished, beholding Thee numbered among the dead, yet destroying the might of death, O Savior, and raising Adam with Thyself, and setting all free from hades.[^10995]

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord:*  
*Troparion 2:*  
Why do ye mingle the myrrh with compassionate tears, O ye women disciples? The angel shining in the tomb said unto the myrrh-bearing women: See ye the tomb, and understand; for the Savior is risen from the tomb.[^10996]

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord:*  
*Troparion 3:*  
Very early in the morning the myrrh-bearing women ran unto Thy tomb weeping; but the angel stood before them and said: The time of lamentation has ceased, weep not; but proclaim the Resurrection unto the Apostles.[^10997]

*Blessed art Thou, O Lord:*  
*Troparion 4:*  
The myrrh-bearing women, having come with myrrh unto Thy tomb, O Savior, lamented; but the angel spoke unto them, saying: Why think ye of the Living One with the dead? For as God He is risen from the tomb.[^10998]

*Glory: Triadicon:*  
Let us worship the Father, and His Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Holy Trinity in one essence, crying with the Seraphim: Holy, Holy, Holy art Thou, O Lord.[^10999]

*Both now: Theotokion:*  
Having given birth unto the Giver of life, O Virgin, thou didst deliver Adam from sin; and joy instead of sorrow didst thou bestow upon Eve; and those fallen from life did He direct unto the same, the God and man Who was incarnate of thee.[^11000]

*Alleluia, thrice. Glory to Thee, O God.*[^11001]

---

[^9507]: First Resurrection Sticheron at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 8.

[^9516]: Eighth Sticheron (First to the Theotokos) at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 8, opening on physical leaf p866 and concluding on physical leaf p867.
[^9517]: Conclusion of the eighth Sticheron (First to the Theotokos), completed from physical leaf p866.

[^9521]: Rubric for the Lucernarium entrance (*Свѣ́те ти́хїй* / *O Gladsome Light*), the Saturday evening Prokeimenon of the day (*The Lord is King*), and the remaining prayers of Vespers.
[^9522]: Rubric for the Resurrection Stichera at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 8 (*На стїхо́внѣ, стїхи̑ры воскре́сны. Гла́съ и҃*).
[^9523]: First Resurrection Sticheron at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 8.
[^9524]: Rubric for the Alphabetical Resurrection Stichera at the Aposticha (*И҆́ны стїхи̑ры, (по а҆лфави́тꙋ)*).

[^9526]: Second Sticheron (Alphabetical) at the Aposticha, Tone 8.
[^9527]: Psalm verse: LXX Ps. 92:1b (*И҆́бо ᲂу҆твердѝ вселе́ннꙋю, ꙗ҆́же не подви́житсѧ* / *For He hath established the world, which shall not be moved*).
[^9528]: Third Sticheron (Alphabetical) at the Aposticha, Tone 8.
[^9529]: Psalm verse: LXX Ps. 92:5 (*До́мꙋ твоемꙋ̀ подоба́етъ свѧты́нѧ Гдⷭ҇и, въ долготꙋ̀ дні́й* / *Holiness becomes Thine house, O Lord, unto length of days*).
[^9530]: Fourth Sticheron (Alphabetical) at the Aposticha, Tone 8, opening on physical leaf p867 and concluding on physical leaf p868.
[^9531]: Conclusion of the fourth Sticheron (Alphabetical) at the Aposticha, completed from physical leaf p867.
[^9532]: Theotokion (Glory... both now...) at the Aposticha of Great Vespers, Tone 8 (*Безневѣ́стнаѧ Дѣ́во*).

[^9709]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 5, Troparion 1. Christ clothed in purple (Matthew 27:28; Mark 15:17) to cover the nakedness of fallen Adam (Genesis 3:7, 21).

[^9725]: Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 8, Ode 6, Troparion 1. Contrast between Adam's outstretched hand in Eden and Christ's hands stretched upon the Cross.

[^9729]: Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 8, Ode 6, Troparion 1. Typological titles of the Virgin Mary: temple, ark of the covenant, animate bridal chamber, and heavenly gate.
[^9730]: Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 8, Ode 6, Troparion 2. 'Idolatrous shrines' translates тре́бищъ (Greek εἰδωλείων), pagan sacrificial altars overthrown by Christ's advent.
[^9731]: Canon to the Most Holy Theotokos, Tone 8, Ode 6, Troparion 3. Marian typology of Jacob's ladder (Genesis 28:12).
[^9732]: Resurrectional Kontakion, Tone 8, modeled on the Podoben 'As first-fruits of nature' (Ὡς ἀπαρχὰς τῆς φύσεως / Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки).
[^9733]: Resurrectional Ikos, Tone 8. Celebrating the harrowing of hades, the proclamation unto the Myrrh-bearers, and the joy of creation.
[^9734]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 7, Heirmos: Θεοῦ συγκατάβασιν τὸ πῦρ ᾐδέσθη ἐν Βαβυλῶνί ποτε... Based on the Song of the Three Holy Children (Daniel 3:49-50).
[^9735]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 7, Troparion 1. The voluntary self-emptying (κένωσις) and poverty of the Son of God on the Cross.
[^9736]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 7, Troparion 2. The deliverance of the souls held captive in hades from the ages (οἱ ἀπ' αἰῶνος δέσμιοι / и҆́же ѿ вѣ́ка ю҆́зницы).
[^9737]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 7, Triadicon. A precise trinitarian dogmatic formulation professing the monarchia of the Father as the sole source of the Son (by eternal generation) and of the upright Spirit (by eternal procession), co-essential and co-eternal.
[^9738]: Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 8, Ode 7, Heirmos: Παῖδες Ἑβραίων ἐν τῇ καμίνῳ / Ἐκ τῆς Ἰουδαίας καταντήσαντες παῖδες ἐν Βαβυλῶνί ποτε...
[^9739]: Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 8, Ode 7, Troparion 1. Cf. Psalm 73:12 [74:12]: 'God is our King before the ages; He hath wrought salvation in the midst of the earth.'
[^9740]: Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 8, Ode 7, Troparion 2.

[^9745]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 8, Heirmos: Ἑπταπλασίως κάμινον τῶν Χαλδαίων ὁ τύραννος... Based on Daniel 3:19-25.

[^9748]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 8, Triadicon. Professing the undivided Trinity: one light, three hypostases, unoriginate Father, co-essential Word, and co-reigning Spirit.
[^9749]: Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 8, Ode 8, Heirmos: Νικηταὶ τυράννου καὶ φλογὸς τῇ χάριτί σου γεγονότες... Based on Daniel 3:57.
[^9750]: Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 8, Ode 8, Troparion 1. 'His comely nakedness' refers to the paradox of Christ's nakedness on the Cross restoring humanity's robe of glory.

[^9759]: Resurrectional Canon, Tone 8, Ode 9, Troparion 1. Chalcedonian Christological confession: Christ, simple in His unoriginate divinity, compounded by the assumption of humanity, suffering as man while impassible as God, in two natures indivisibly and unconfusedly (ἀδιαιρέτως καὶ ἀσυγχύτως / нераздѣльно и неслїянно).

[^9762]: Canon of the Cross and Resurrection, Tone 8, Ode 9, Heirmos: Ἔφριξε πᾶσα ἀκοὴ τὴν ἀπόρρητον Θεοῦ συγκατάβασιν...

[^10931]: Third troparion (Prophets, Holy Women, and Righteous) of Ode 8 of the first Canon in Tone 8, entreating mercy through their collective prayers.

[^10933]: Doxological troparion (Glory, For the Departed) of Ode 8 of the first Canon in Tone 8, appealing to the Righteous Judge to preserve the departed uncondemned.
[^10934]: Theotokion (Both now) of Ode 8 of the first Canon in Tone 8, invoking the Mother of the Sun of Righteousness to dispel the darkness of the soul.
[^10935]: Heirmos of Ode 8 of the second Canon (for the Departed) in Tone 8, describing the musical instruments and idol in the plain of Dura (Daniel 3:1-7) resisted by the three youths.
[^10936]: First troparion (Martyrion) of Ode 8 of the second Canon in Tone 8, praising the martyrs who finished earthly combats and received heavenly crowns.
[^10937]: Second troparion of Ode 8 of the second Canon in Tone 8, confessing Christ's descent into the nethermost pit to resurrect the dead.
[^10938]: Doxological troparion (Glory) of Ode 8 of the second Canon in Tone 8, addressing Christ as the fountain of eternal life and stream of delight.
[^10939]: Theotokion (Both now) of Ode 8 of the second Canon in Tone 8, invoking the Virgin Mother of God the Savior.
[^10940]: Rubric prescribing the chanting of the Canticle of the Theotokos (Magnificat, Luke 1:46-55: *Вели́читъ дꙋша̀ моѧ̀ Гдⷭа*) before Ode 9.
[^10941]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the first Canon in Tone 8, magnifying the unwedded Mother of God exalted above the heavenly powers.
[^10942]: First troparion (Martyrs) of Ode 9 of the first Canon in Tone 8, praising the martyrs standing before unjust judges and winning crowns of righteousness.
[^10943]: Second troparion (Hierarchs) of Ode 9 of the first Canon in Tone 8, likening the holy bishops and pastors to the helms of the Church guiding the spiritual vessel unto the harbor of the commandments.
[^10944]: Third troparion (Prophets, Venerables, and Holy Women) of Ode 9 of the first Canon in Tone 8, invoking their collective intercession for divine compassions.
[^10945]: Doxological troparion (Glory, For the Departed) of Ode 9 of the first Canon in Tone 8, praying that the departed may partake of eternal honor and glory.
[^10946]: Theotokion (Both now) of Ode 9 of the first Canon in Tone 8, asking the all-immaculate Mother carrying all good things in her arms to guide our way toward God.
[^10947]: Heirmos of Ode 9 of the second Canon (for the Departed) in Tone 8, meditating with awe upon the ineffable condescension of the Most High taking flesh from the Virgin.
[^10948]: First troparion (Martyrs) of Ode 9 of the second Canon in Tone 8, extolling the martyrs illuminated by Trinity-rays who defied the tyrants' decrees.
[^10949]: Second troparion of Ode 9 of the second Canon in Tone 8, praising the destruction of bitter hades and petitioning unwaning light for the departed.
[^10950]: Doxological troparion (Glory) of Ode 9 of the second Canon in Tone 8, contemplating Christ as all sweetness, all desire, and ineffable beauty, and praying for the departed to enjoy His comeliness.
[^10951]: Theotokion (Both now) of Ode 9 of the second Canon in Tone 8, dogmatically professing Christ as twofold in nature, only-begotten of the Father and firstborn of creation from the Virgin.
[^10952]: Rubric prescribing "It is truly meet" (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), the Little Litany, the Exapostilarion, and the customary morning psalms of the Praises (Psalms 148-150 LXX).
[^10953]: Rubric introducing the Stichera of the Martyrs at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8. The printed Slavonic text errantly reads *Гла́съ г҃.* (Tone 3) due to a recurring typographical defect where the letter *и҃* (8) was typeset or damaged as *г҃* (3); the hymns and melodic models follow Tone 8 throughout.
[^10954]: First Sticheron of the Martyrs at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8 (*Что̀ ва́съ нарече́мъ, Ст҃і́и?...*), addressing the martyrs through typological comparisons to Cherubim, Seraphim, Angels, and Powers.
[^10955]: Second Sticheron of the Martyrs at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8, praising their public confession before kings and their ongoing healings in the world.
[^10956]: Third Sticheron of the Martyrs at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8, commemorating their victory over deception through the Cross, spanning across physical leaves p989 and p990.
[^10957]: Conclusion of the third Sticheron of the Martyrs at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8, continuing from physical leaf p989.
[^10958]: Fourth Sticheron of the Martyrs at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8, depicting the martyrs clad in the breastplates of faith and armed with the sign of the Cross.
[^10959]: Doxological Sticheron (Glory) at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8, presenting a sobering contemplation of the unmerciful Judge, the gnashing of teeth, outer darkness, and eternal fire, while praying for rest for the elect.
[^10960]: Theotokion (Both now) at the Praises on Saturday in Tone 8, invoking the pure Virgin as the Gate of the Word.
[^10961]: Rubric introducing the Aposticha Stichera for the Departed at Saturday Matins in Tone 8.
[^10962]: Melodic model heading (*Podoben*) in Tone 8, referencing the automelon "O most marvelous wonder" (*Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ*).
[^10963]: First Sticheron for the Departed at the Aposticha on Saturday in Tone 8 (*Ца́рское мнѣ̀ подписа́лъ є҆сѝ свобожде́ніе...*), meditating on Christ signing our charter of liberty with the scarlet ink of His own blood.
[^10964]: First psalm verse at the Aposticha of Saturday Matins (Psalm 64:5 LXX: *Блаже́ни, ꙗ҆̀же и҆збра́лъ, и҆ прія́лъ є҆сѝ Гдⷭи*).
[^10965]: Second Sticheron for the Departed at the Aposticha on Saturday in Tone 8 (*Свѧще́нствовавъ ꙗ҆́кѡ человѣ́къ...*), portraying Christ offering Himself as Priest and Lamb unto the Father, and praying for the departed to dwell beside the streams of delight.
[^10966]: Second psalm verse at the Aposticha of Saturday Matins (Psalm 24:13 LXX: *Дꙋ́ши и҆́хъ во благи́хъ водворѧ́тсѧ*).
[^10967]: Third Sticheron for the Departed at the Aposticha on Saturday in Tone 8 (*Глꙋбино́ю мꙋ́дрости...*), invoking Christ's providence over human life and petitioning rest beside the waters of repose (Psalm 22:2 LXX).
[^10968]: Third psalm verse at the Aposticha of Saturday Matins (Psalm 101:13 / 111:6 LXX: *И҆ па́мѧть и҆́хъ въ ро́дъ и҆ ро́дъ*).
[^10969]: Fourth Sticheron for the Departed at the Aposticha on Saturday in Tone 8 (*Сло́во сы́й неви́димый...*), confessing the invisible Word co-originate with the Father and the Spirit, spanning across physical leaves p990 and p991.
[^10970]: Conclusion of Sticheron 4 for the Departed at the Aposticha of Saturday Matins in Tone 8, continuing from physical leaf p990.
[^10971]: Doxological Theotokion (*Сла́ва: и҆ ны́нѣ: Бг҃оро́диченъ*) at the Aposticha of Saturday Matins in Tone 8 (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ заче́нши безнача́льное Сло́во...*), petitioning the Mother of God to settle her servants where the righteous keep unending festival.
[^10972]: Rubric introducing the Beatitudes (*Блаже́нна*) at the Divine Liturgy on Saturday in Tone 8.
[^10973]: Opening troparion / Beatitude verse at the Divine Liturgy on Saturday in Tone 8 (*Поманѝ на́съ Хрⷭтѐ Сп҃се мі́ра...*), invoking the prayer of the wise thief upon the tree.
[^10974]: First troparion (Martyrs) of the Beatitudes on Saturday in Tone 8, extolling the passion-bearers who wounded demonic hosts through their holy wounds.
[^10975]: Second troparion (Hierarchs, Prophets, and Venerables) of the Beatitudes on Saturday in Tone 8, praising the saints who attained the heavenly inheritance.
[^10976]: Third troparion (For the Departed) of the Beatitudes on Saturday in Tone 8, praying for the faithful departed to inherit paradise.
[^10977]: Doxological troparion (Glory) of the Beatitudes on Saturday in Tone 8, offering a final Trinitarian hymn for the rest of the departed and mercy for the singer.
[^10978]: Theotokion (Both now) of the Beatitudes on Saturday in Tone 8, petitioning the Virgin Mother of all goodness for remission of sins and great mercy.
[^10979]: Colophon rubric marking the completion of the entire Tone 8 weekly cycle (*Коне́цъ ѻ҆смо́мꙋ Гла́сꙋ*).
[^10980]: Title and liturgical rubric introducing the Triadica of St. Gregory of Sinai (*Тро́йчни Григо́рїа Сїнаі́та*), appointed to be chanted on all Sundays after the Triadic Canon at the Midnight Office (*на Полꙋ́нощницѣ*).
[^10981]: First Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Досто́йно є҆́сть бг҃олѣ́пнѡ сла́вити тѧ̀...*), confessing the Holy Trinity as boundless, transcendent, Monad in essence and three in hypostases.
[^10982]: Second Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Господонача́льнꙋю Херꙋвї́мъ...*), exalting the Trinity as Master-Origin of the Cherubim and Seraphim.
[^10983]: Third Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Ѻ҆тцꙋ̀ безначáльнꙋ и҆ Бг҃ꙋ...*), worshipping the unoriginate Father, co-unoriginate Word, and the Spirit in inseparable essence.
[^10984]: Fourth Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Воспои́мъ всѝ бг҃олѣ́пнѡ...*), offering divine songs unto the three-hypostatic dominion and one Kingdom.
[^10985]: Fifth Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Лꙋчеза̑рныѧ твоѧ̑ мѡ́лнїа возсїѧ́й мнѣ̀...*), petitioning the Creator of all for the radiant lightnings of divine grace.
[^10986]: Sixth Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Є҆го́же трепе́щꙋтъ и҆ трѧсꙋ́тсѧ Херꙋвї́ми...*), praising Christ incarnate of the Virgin before Whom the heavenly powers quake.
[^10987]: Seventh Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Досто́йно є҆́сть всеблагоговѣ́йнѡ моли́ти тѧ̀...*), praying with tears unto the All-Merciful Savior.
[^10988]: Eighth Triadicon of Gregory of Sinai (*Прїимѝ сле́зы Царю̀...*), begging the King of all for deliverance from the flame and outer darkness and a portion in the heavenly Kingdom.
[^10989]: Doxological Triadicon (Glory) of Gregory of Sinai (*Ѻ҆́тче Вседержи́телю, Сло́ве Бо́жїй...*), confessing the one power and godhead of the Trinity, spanning across physical leaves p992 and p993.
[^10990]: Conclusion of the doxological Triadicon (Glory) of Gregory of Sinai, continuing from physical leaf p992.
[^10991]: Theotokion (Both now) of the Triadica of Gregory of Sinai (*И҆зъ ме́ртвыхъ ви́дѣвши твоего̀ Сы́на...*), contemplating the joy of creation at Christ's Resurrection.
[^10992]: Rubric concluding the Sunday Midnight Office, prescribing "It is truly meet" (*Досто́йно є҆́сть*), Trisagion, Our Father, the Hypakoe of the Tone or the penitential troparia (*Поми́лꙋй на́съ Гдⷭи*), litany, and dismissal.
[^10993]: Rubric introducing the Resurrectional Evlogitaria (*Тропарѝ пѣва́еми по Непоро́чнахъ по всѧ̑ Недѣ́ли*), chanted after the 17th Kathisma (The Blameless / *Непоро́чны*, Psalm 118 LXX) at Sunday Matins in Tone 5.
[^10994]: Scriptural refrain (*Благослове́нъ є҆сѝ Гдⷭи, наꙋчи́ мѧ ѡ҆правда́нїємъ твои̑мъ*) taken from Psalm 118:12 LXX, chanted before each Evlogitarion troparion.
[^10995]: First Evlogitarion troparion in Tone 5 (*А҆́гг҃льскїй собо́ръ ᲂу҆диви́сѧ...*), recounting the astonishment of the angelic host at Christ's descent into hades and the raising of Adam.
[^10996]: Second Evlogitarion troparion in Tone 5 (*По что̀ мѵ́ра со ми́лостивными слеза́ми...*), depicting the radiant angel at the tomb asking the women why they mingle myrrh with tears.
[^10997]: Third Evlogitarion troparion in Tone 5 (*Зѣлѡ̀ ра́нѡ, мѵроно́сицы теча́хꙋ...*), showing the angel announcing that weeping has ceased and commanding them to proclaim the Resurrection to the Apostles.
[^10998]: Fourth Evlogitarion troparion in Tone 5 (*Мѵроно́сицы жены̀ съ мѵ́ры прише́дшїѧ...*), recording the angelic declaration: "Why seek ye the Living One among the dead?"
[^10999]: Doxological troparion (Glory: Triadicon) of the Evlogitaria in Tone 5 (*Поклони́мсѧ Ѻ҆тцꙋ̀...*), confessing the Holy Trinity in one essence with the Seraphic hymn.
[^11000]: Theotokion (Both now) of the Evlogitaria in Tone 5 (*Жизнода́вца ро́ждши...*), praising the Mother of God who delivered Adam from sin and gave joy unto Eve.
[^11001]: Concluding triple Alleluia and doxology (*А҆ллилꙋ́їа, г҃. Сла́ва тебѣ̀ Бо́же*) sealing the Sunday Evlogitaria.
